# Roadmap — BillWatch
**Maturity:** HIGH (live, web+iOS+Android). **Wedge:** simpler/cheaper *federal-only* consumer tracker vs FastDemocracy. **Stopping point:** federal tracking + digests + alerts + AI summaries, mobile at parity → maintain; don't chase 50-state coverage.

## P1 — the consumer wedge (next ~2 quarters)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Weekly digest (grouped by stage/topic) + endpoint + view {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Real-time alerts on tracked topics/bills (rules + match) {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Plain-English "what changed" status summaries {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
## P2 — mobile parity + polish (~q3-4)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] iOS/Android feature parity with web (tracking, digests, alerts) — researched 2026-09-09: both apps are NATIVE (Swift/SwiftUI iOS, Kotlin Android — not Flutter/cross-platform as earlier notes assumed). They've already DIVERGED from EACH OTHER, not just from web: iOS has AICoachingView/AdvancedAnalyticsView/CareerHistoryTimelineView/LegislatorFinanceView/RecommendationsView that Android lacks; Android has BillTrackingScreen/AdvancedFilterScreen/PaywallScreen/TopicsScreen that iOS lacks. This is a 3-way gap (mobile-vs-mobile as much as mobile-vs-web), not a simple "port web to mobile." First real task: audit each of the ~30 backend routers + ~19 web views against what each native app actually consumes, then close mobile-vs-mobile gaps first (cheaper, both apps already have the muscle memory) before mobile-vs-web. {cat: mobile; size: L; research: web}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Legislator profiles + scorecards surface polish {cat: web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Committee hearing & floor-activity calendar for tracked bills — Surface a "This Week in Congress" view showing scheduled committee hearings/markups for bills a user follows, pulled from Congress.gov's documented `/committee-meeting` endpoint (filterable by chamber/date range, already the same data source BillWatch's `committee_sync_service` and `committees` router consume). Fits the wedge: federal-only, single unified calendar (not 50-state schedules), and it's a natural next hop from committee data already synced rather than new infrastructure. Ties directly into existing alert/digest plumbing (a hearing becomes another alert-worthy "what changed" event). {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: github.com/LibraryOfCongress/api.congress.gov CommitteeMeetingEndpoint.md, congress.gov/help/committee-schedule-meetings-hearings}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Shareable bill status cards — One-tap "share" on any tracked bill generates a branded image/link card (bill number, plain-English status line, latest vote tally, BillWatch logo) for text/social, reusing the existing plain-English status-summary text (`status_change_summary.py`) and vote data (`models/vote.py`) already in the DB — no new data source, just an OG-image/render endpoint plus a share button in the 3 clients. Fits the wedge as a near-zero-cost, high-leverage growth loop for a solo dev (free user acquisition vs. FastDemocracy's sales-led enterprise motion) without adding any tracking scope beyond federal bills already followed. {cat: backend+web; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: FastDemocracy.com feature review (G2/SourceForge), GovTrack.us feature survey}
## P3 — depth (~year 2)
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Campaign-finance / vote-record insights as a light premium tier — CORRECTED 2026-09-09: this was tagged needs-research but the core work is ALREADY BUILT — `app/services/fec_api.py` is a real OpenFEC client (api.open.fec.gov), with `campaign_finance_sync_service.py`, `models/campaign_finance.py`, and a router already exposing `/legislator/{id}/finance` + `/disclosures` (tested: tests/test_fec_api.py). The ONLY missing piece is the "premium tier" gate itself — no premium/subscription/tier check currently exists on these endpoints. Decomposes into: (1) add a premium-tier check to the finance/disclosures endpoints (reuse whatever gate billing.py/PremiumView already uses elsewhere), (2) a light paywall/blur treatment on LegislatorFinanceView (iOS) for non-premium users. Small, NOT a research item. {cat: backend+web; size: S (was M); research: none — verified against current code 2026-09-09}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Plain-English bill "odds of passage" indicator — A lightweight, transparent heuristic (not ML) scoring a bill's chance of advancing, based on stage, days-since-introduction, committee, and historical base rates computed from BillWatch's own synced bill-history data (`bill_sync_service`, `bill_stage.py`), presented as a plain-language line ("similar bills at this stage became law ~12% of the time") consistent with BillWatch's existing "what changed" plain-English framing. Deliberately simpler than GovTrack's regression-based Prognosis model to keep it cheap to build/maintain solo, while still closing a visible gap vs. GovTrack. Federal-only and additive to existing bill-detail/digest surfaces, no new external data source required. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: GovTrack.us "New on GovTrack: Bill Prognosis" + "Even Better Bill Prognosis" methodology posts}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] "Bills like this" similarity finder — Surface companion/duplicate/related bills (e.g. House and Senate versions of the same policy) on a bill's detail page, using lightweight embedding similarity over bill titles/AI summaries already generated by the existing `llm_service`/`bill_summary_service` pipeline — no new AI vendor or infra, just an embedding step added to a summary job that already runs. Matches GovTrack's well-liked "Find Similar Bills" feature but stays federal-only and reuses paid-for AI calls already in the budget rather than adding a new integration surface. {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: GovTrack.us "Find Similar Bills" feature docs}

## Newly discovered (2026-09-14 refuel — roadmap was dry)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix crash bug in POST/PATCH /api/alerts (duplicate broken alert system) — app/routers/alerts.py's create_alert/update_alert call app/services/alert_service.py's AlertService.create_alert/update_alert, which construct BillAlert(..., sponsor_states=..., ...) and later set alert.notify_on_match, but app/models/analytics.py's BillAlert (lines 57-80) has no sponsor_states or notify_on_match columns — only keywords/policy_areas/chambers/bill_types/notify_on_status_change/notify_on_action/notify_on_vote/is_active/created_at/last_checked_at — so BillAlert(sponsor_states=...) raises TypeError on every authenticated POST /api/alerts; get_user_alerts also calls selectinload(BillAlert.matched_bills), a relationship that doesn't exist anywhere on the model (verified via grep of app/models/analytics.py and app/models/bill.py). This is a second, independent alert system living alongside the working app/routers/analytics.py /api/analytics/alerts endpoints (app/services/bill_alert_service.py's BillAlertService, which correctly matches the real model columns and is schema-validated via app/schemas/analytics.py's BillAlertCreateRequest). Verified via grep that tests/test_alert_service.py only covers the unrelated pure function should_trigger_hearing_alert, and tests/test_mounted_routers.py only checks the unauthenticated-401 case and route registration for /api/alerts — never an authenticated create — so this crash is 100% uncaught today. Fix alert_service.py to only set real BillAlert columns (drop sponsor_states/notify_on_match, use chambers like bill_alert_service.py does) and remove/fix the matched_bills selectinload, then add an authenticated create/update router test. {cat:backend; size:M; multifile:yes; research:none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix crash bugs in GET /api/votes/comparison and GET /api/votes/statistics/{bioguide_id} — app/routers/votes.py's compare_legislator_votes queries `select(Vote)...order_by(Vote.vote_date.desc())` and reads `v.congress_id`/`v.stance`, and get_voting_statistics reads `v.stance` in list comprehensions (`sum(1 for v in votes if v.stance == "yes")` etc.), but app/models/vote.py's Vote model only has id/roll_call_id/legislator_id/position — no vote_date, congress_id, or stance column (vote_date lives on the related RollCallVote, reached via a join). The file's own header comment (votes.py lines 18-22) already documents this exact pitfall and defines a `_stance(position)` helper plus a RollCallVote join, which get_legislator_votes and get_bill_votes correctly use — compare_legislator_votes and get_voting_statistics were simply never updated to follow it. Verified via grep that no test anywhere invokes these two endpoints with real vote data (tests/test_mounted_routers.py only checks /api/votes/comparison's route registration, no functional call). Fix both to join RollCallVote for vote_date and use `_stance(v.position)` in place of `v.stance`/`v.congress_id`, then add router tests with seeded Vote/RollCallVote rows. {cat:backend; size:M; multifile:yes; research:none}

## Newly discovered (2026-09-19 refuel — roadmap fully [decomposed], no [ready] items left)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix Bill Chat "send message" always failing with 401 for logged-in users — billwatch-web/src/stores/billChat.ts and src/components/BillChatPanel.vue both `import axios from 'axios'` directly and call the raw client (`axios.post(\`/api/bills/${billId}/chat\`, ...)`), instead of the shared src/services/api.ts client. Verified: the shared `api` client's request interceptor is the ONLY place that attaches `Authorization: Bearer <token>` from localStorage (app/services/api.ts lines 11-17); raw `axios.*` calls never go through it. The backend endpoint `POST /{bill_id}/chat` (app/routers/bill_chat.py line 28) requires `Depends(get_current_user)` (hard auth, not `get_current_user_optional` like the GET at line 82) — so every send-message request from these two files reaches the backend with no Authorization header and gets a 401. This bug survives even the Vercel `/api/:path*` rewrite (vercel.json) since it's an auth failure, not a routing failure. Fix: switch both files to `import api from '../services/api'` and use `api.post('/bills/${billId}/chat', ...)` (relative path, no leading `/api`, matching every other store's convention). {cat:web; size:S; multifile:yes; research:none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix double `/api/api/...` 404s breaking global search and legislator search — billwatch-web/src/pages/SearchPage.vue (`api.get('/api/search/advanced', ...)`) and src/components/LegislatorSearch.vue (`api.get('/api/legislators/search', ...)`) both call the shared `api` client (src/services/api.ts) whose `baseURL` is `VITE_API_URL` = `https://billwatch-production.up.railway.app/api` in production (verified in billwatch-web/.env.production) — then ALSO prepend a literal `/api/` to the call path. The resulting request goes to `.../api/api/search/advanced` and `.../api/api/legislators/search`, which don't exist: the real routes are mounted at `/api/search` (`app.include_router(search.router, prefix="/api/search", ...)`, main.py line 117) and `/api/legislators` (main.py line 101). Unlike the raw-axios bug above, this one is NOT saved by the Vercel rewrite, because the `api` client's baseURL is already the full absolute Railway URL — the request never touches the Vercel origin the rewrite intercepts. Fix: drop the leading `/api` from both call sites (`api.get('/search/advanced', ...)`, `api.get('/legislators/search', ...)`). {cat:web; size:S; multifile:yes; research:none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix the same double `/api/api/...` 404 bug breaking the admin dashboard — billwatch-web/src/pages/Admin.vue calls `api.get('/api/admin/users')` and `api.get('/api/admin/statistics')` through the same shared `api` client described above, producing `.../api/api/admin/users` against a backend that only registers `/api/admin/...` (app/routers/admin.py line 14: `router = APIRouter(prefix="/api/admin", ...)`, mounted with no extra prefix in main.py line 118). Same root cause and same fix pattern as the search bug above (drop the leading `/api`), called out separately since it's a distinct file/feature (admin-only, lower blast radius than the two P1 items). {cat:web; size:S; multifile:no; research:none}

## Newly discovered (2026-09-20 refuel — roadmap fully [decomposed], 0 backlog items left; Claude research pass)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Wire up production error monitoring/crash reporting across all 3 clients — `sentry-sdk[fastapi]>=1.45.0` has been listed in `billwatch-backend/requirements.txt` but is never imported or initialized anywhere (confirmed via repo-wide grep for `sentry_sdk`/`SENTRY_DSN`/`sentry.init` in billwatch-backend — zero hits outside the dependency line itself). The backend already has real observability plumbing built around the gap it leaves: structured root-logger config (`app/core/logging.py`) and per-request correlation ids (`app/core/request_id_middleware.py`, `X-Request-ID` stamped on every log line and response header) — but no actual exception capture. The two `except Exception` catch-alls in `app/main.py` (the `/health` DB-check failure path and the `/metrics` psutil failure path) just log/swallow; nothing durable records real user-facing errors from any of the ~30 routers or the APScheduler jobs. None of the 3 live client platforms have any crash-reporting SDK either (confirmed via grep for "sentry"/"crashlytics" across billwatch-web, billwatch-ios, and billwatch-android — zero hits in all three). Net effect: this is a live, revenue-generating production app (real users, real Stripe billing) with zero visibility into real-world errors beyond whatever happens to scroll past in Railway's stdout logs. Scope: (1) backend — add a `sentry_dsn: str = ""` field to `Settings`, call `sentry_sdk.init(dsn=..., integrations=[...])` in the app factory gated on its presence (same "empty string = no-op" pattern already used for other optional integrations in this codebase), and confirm an unhandled exception in a router is actually captured with environment/release tags; (2) web — add `@sentry/vue` (or a minimal `app.config.errorHandler` + `window.onerror` capture posting to Sentry's browser SDK) gated on a `VITE_SENTRY_DSN` env var, matching the "unset = no-op" pattern; (3) iOS/Android — flag as a follow-up rather than force it into this same pass (adding a native crash-reporting dependency to two separate mobile build systems is a bigger, riskier change than the backend/web piece and deserves its own scoped pass once the web+backend half proves out the pattern). {cat: backend+web; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}

## Newly discovered (2026-09-21 refuel — roadmap fully [decomposed], 0 backlog items left; Claude research pass, production robustness beyond monitoring)
- [x] [P1] [done] The GDPR/CCPA "export my data" and "delete my account" endpoints are 100% fake — `app/services/data_export_service.py`'s `DataExportService` (all 4 methods: `request_export`/`get_export_status`/`list_exports`/`delete_account`) never touches the DB or any real storage: `request_export` returns a hardcoded `{"status": "processing", ...}` dict with no persistence; `get_export_status` unconditionally returns `{"status": "completed", "download_url": f"https://cdn.billwatch.app/exports/{export_id}.zip", ...}` regardless of whether that export_id was ever requested (the CDN URL doesn't exist — verified via repo-wide grep for `cdn.billwatch.app`, zero references anywhere else); `list_exports` returns two literal hardcoded fake rows for every user; and `delete_account` returns `{"status": "deleted", "data_wiped": True, "message": "All data has been permanently deleted"}` while never issuing a single DELETE against any table — confirmed via `grep -n 'db\.' app/services/data_export_service.py` returning zero hits, the service takes no db session at all. `tests/test_data_export_service.py` only asserts against this same hardcoded fake shape (e.g. `assert result['data_wiped'] is True`), so this has never been caught. This sits directly behind the already-tracked P1 crash bug elsewhere in this roadmap (`current_user['id']` subscript on a `User` ORM object) — once that crash is fixed, every authenticated call to `POST /api/export/export`, `GET /api/export/exports(/{id})`, and `DELETE /api/export/account` will succeed and claim success while doing nothing real. No web/iOS/Android client currently calls any of these four endpoints either (confirmed via repo-wide grep for `delete.*account`/`deleteAccount`/`/api/export` across billwatch-web/src, billwatch-ios/Sources, billwatch-android/app/src/main — the only hit is static privacy copy on TermsPage.vue), so this is a live production app whose privacy-policy-promised data rights are currently unimplementable even manually. Scope: rewrite `DataExportService` to take a DB session and (1) `request_export`/`get_export_status` build a real export by querying the user's own rows (bill follows, topics, alerts, saved searches, watch/notification history) and persist an export record + real status, or at minimum synchronously assemble and return the real data rather than a fake async job; (2) `delete_account` actually deletes/anonymizes the `User` row and cascades their owned rows (follows, alerts, saved searches, device tokens, etc.) in a real transaction; (3) rewrite `tests/test_data_export_service.py` to assert against a seeded DB fixture instead of the hardcoded shape; (4) add a real "Delete my account" control reachable in at least one client (web `ProfileView.vue` is the natural home, next to the existing address-save flow) — Apple Guideline 5.1.1(v) already requires in-app account deletion for any app with account creation, independent of this bug. {cat: backend+web; size: L; multifile: yes; research: none} <!-- SUPERSEDED 2026-09-21: real fix implemented and PR'd by interactive Claude session (branch fix/gdpr-delete-account-real-deletion, PR #29 -> develop): instance-based DataExportService with real cascading delete_account, real request_export/get_export_status backed by a new export_records table + 0004_add_export_records.py migration, real DB-state tests. Do not decompose or stage further work on this item -- it duplicates/conflicts with PR #29's architecture (e.g. this queue's own second auto-refill created a competing export_request.py model + 0004_add_export_request_table.py migration with a colliding revision id). Any already-committed overnight/feature work on data_export_service.py from this item should be reconciled against PR #29 by a human/Claude reviewer when that PR merges, not auto-merged over it. -->

- [ ] [P1] Fix crash bug in GET /api/activity-logs/logs — app/routers/activity_logs.py's get_activity_logs (line 22-23) type-hints `current_user: dict = Depends(get_current_user)` and subscripts `current_user["id"]`, but get_current_user (app/routers/auth.py, `async def get_current_user(...) -> User`) returns a real `User` SQLAlchemy ORM instance with no `__getitem__` — every authenticated call to GET /api/activity-logs/logs raises `TypeError: 'User' object is not subscriptable` in production. This is the SAME bug pattern already tracked for app/routers/data_export.py elsewhere in this roadmap, but in a DIFFERENT, previously-unflagged file (verified via direct read of activity_logs.py, not assumption). tests/test_activity_logs_router.py's `test_get_activity_logs` hides this completely: it overrides `get_current_user` with `async def override_get_current_user(): return {"id": "test-user-id", "workspace_id": "test-workspace"}` — a plain dict, not a real `User` — so the subscript never fails in the test even though it always would in production (verified by reading the fixture directly). Separately, `ActivityLoggingService.get_activity_log` and `.log_activity` (app/services/activity_logging_service.py, 67 lines) are ALSO both 100% hardcoded fake stubs (`"total_entries": 1250`, two canned `{"action": "view"/"follow", ...}` entries) that never touch the DB — the exact same fake-stub pattern the `/audit-report` endpoint in this same router already had fixed (migrated to the real `SecurityAuditService`, verified in the same file) — `/logs` was simply missed in that migration. Fix: change `current_user: dict` to `current_user: User` and `current_user["id"]` to `current_user.id`, rewrite the test to authenticate via a real registered user + JWT (the `valid_token`-fixture pattern already used in tests/test_follow_bills.py) instead of a dict override, and either wire `/logs` to `SecurityAuditService` (matching `/audit-report`) or explicitly scope `ActivityLoggingService.get_activity_log` to real DB-backed activity if that's a distinct concern. {cat:backend; size:S; multifile:yes; research:none}

## Refuel 2026-09-25 (Claude research pass — roadmap was fully [decomposed]/dry)

All research is grounded in the live repo.

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] **[LIVE BUG]** Fix `bill_status.py`'s `STATUS_ALIASES` to recognize the literal status strings `congress_api.py` actually writes to `bill.status`, breaking the real-time alert threshold check silently — `CongressAPIClient.determine_bill_status` (`app/services/congress_api.py:443-476`, called from `bill_sync_service.py:212` and persisted directly to `bill.status` at line 223) returns underscored literals `passed_house`/`passed_senate`/`to_president`/`became_law`/`vetoed`, but `app/utils/bill_status.py`'s `STATUS_ALIASES` (lines 15-28) only maps the SPACED variants (`"passed house"`, `"passed senate"`) to `passed_chamber`, and has no entry at all for `to_president`, `became_law`, or `vetoed` (`STATUS_ORDER`, lines 6-11, also has no `vetoed` slot). Since `normalize_status()` only lowercases/strips (no space/underscore normalization), a real production `bill.status` of `"passed_house"` falls through unmatched and `status_rank()` returns -1 for it — verified by reading both files directly. This -1 silently breaks `should_alert()` (`app/utils/alert_threshold.py`: `new_rank > old_rank` becomes `-1 > 1` = False) for real House/Senate passage and veto transitions on the flagship "real-time alerts" feature, and also skews `digest.py`/`digest_grouping.py`'s "most-advanced-first" ordering. Confirmed uncaught: every test in `tests/test_alert_threshold.py` and `tests/test_alert_priority.py` exercises only the spaced/aliased forms (`"Passed House"`, `"Signed Into Law"`) or the already-correct `passed_chamber`/`enacted`, never the real underscored value the sync job actually stores. Fix: add `passed_house`/`passed_senate` (underscore) to `STATUS_ALIASES` alongside the existing spaced keys, add `to_president`/`vetoed` as real rank slots (vetoed as a terminal/dead-end rank, not below `enacted`), and map `became_law` as an alias to `enacted`; add a regression test using the literal strings `determine_bill_status` actually returns. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] **[LIVE BUG]** Fix `recommendation_service.py`'s stale "Active"/"Introduced" status literals silently disabling all personalized bill recommendations — `RecommendationService.recommend_bills_for_user` (`app/services/recommendation_service.py:66`) filters candidate bills with `.where(Bill.status.in_(["Active", "Introduced"]))`, but `bill.status` is always written in lowercase snake_case by `determine_bill_status()` (`introduced`, `in_committee`, `passed_house`, etc. — confirmed via `bill_sync_service.py:223` and `congress_api.py:443-476`) — `"Active"` is not a status this app has ever produced, and `"Introduced"` (capital I) never case-matches the stored `"introduced"` under Postgres's default case-sensitive comparison. This means the topic/committee-based match clause (lines 55-64, built from the user's real followed_topics/followed_committees) always returns zero rows against real data, so the code always falls through to `self._trending_excluding(...)` (line 68-69) — every user's "recommended for you" bills are silently just generic trending bills, never actually personalized, regardless of what they follow. Confirmed uncaught: `tests/test_recommendation_service.py`'s `_bill()` fixture helper (line 33) defaults `status="Active"` to match the buggy code, hiding the mismatch from every test in the file. Fix: change the filter to the real lowercase values (e.g. `["introduced", "in_committee", "passed_house", "passed_senate"]`, or reuse `bill_status.status_rank` to select "not yet enacted/dead"), and update the test fixture default to a real status value so a future regression is caught. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Alert the founder when a Congress.gov ingestion source goes stale, instead of only exposing it to end users as a passive badge — `app/services/sync_status_service.py`'s `is_stale()` (lines 68-72) and `staleness_threshold()` (lines 62-66) already compute exactly this signal, but the ONLY consumer anywhere in the codebase is `app/routers/sync_status.py:19` feeding a `DataFreshnessBadge.vue` UI badge (verified via repo-wide grep for `is_stale`/`staleness` — zero hits outside that one router + the service itself). For an app whose entire value proposition is "accurate legislative data," a Congress.gov outage or API-key expiry (both already handled gracefully as retryable errors in `bill_sync_service.py`, but not alarmed on) could go unnoticed for days with nothing but a quiet badge color change — nobody is watching Railway logs 24/7 on a solo-founder app. `app/services/email_service.py` (SendGrid-API-first/SMTP-fallback, already used by `digest_service.py` and `status_change_alert_service.py`) is the natural existing mechanism to reuse. Add a scheduled job (mirroring the existing job-registration pattern in `app/jobs/scheduler.py`) that checks `sync_status_service.get_all_statuses()` against `is_stale()` for each of the 3 KNOWN_SOURCES and emails a configured admin address (a new `admin_alert_email` setting, empty-string-is-no-op like other optional settings) once a source crosses the threshold, deduped so it doesn't re-fire every run while still stale. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] **[HUMAN/design]** Add a real "bill died / session ended" terminal status instead of leaving dead bills stuck at their last real stage forever — verified via direct read of `app/services/congress_api.py`'s `determine_bill_status` (lines 443-476) and `app/utils/bill_status.py`'s `STATUS_ORDER`/`STATUS_ALIASES` that neither has ANY concept of a bill dying at the end of a two-year Congress (the fate of the vast majority — historically ~90%+ — of introduced bills never gets a distinct terminal state; such a bill just silently stops receiving new actions and sits at whatever stage it last reached, e.g. `in_committee`, indefinitely). A user tracking a bill that died in 2025 sees it forever as "in committee" with no signal it's over, which is a direct hit against the "core promise is accurate legislative data" and against the already-shipped "plain-English what changed" framing (a bill going dead IS a status change worth surfacing). This needs a human/Claude design call before decomposing into 27B-sized tasks: the detection heuristic isn't a clean API field — it likely needs "no action in N months AND the Congress that introduced it has since ended" (Congress sessions are known 2-year windows, `congress_number` already exists on `Bill` per `bill_stage.py`/`congress_number.py`) rather than anything Congress.gov returns directly, so the exact threshold/logic deserves a deliberate decision rather than an LLM guess. Once decided, the pipeline is straightforward: new terminal status (e.g. `died`) in `STATUS_ORDER`, a periodic job (same scheduler pattern as `sync_votes_job`) to sweep old-Congress bills below `enacted` and mark them, and a status-change alert/digest entry for the transition (reusing `status_change_summary.py`'s existing plain-English framing). {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: needed — human/Claude judgment call on the death-detection heuristic before decomposing}

- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Prune invalid/unregistered FCM device tokens after failed push sends instead of retrying them forever — `NotificationService.send_notification` (`app/services/notification_service.py:116-125`) calls `messaging.send_each(messages)` and reads only `response.success_count` to decide overall success, but never inspects the per-message `response.responses[i].exception` results to detect Firebase's `UNREGISTERED`/invalid-token errors (the standard signal that a device uninstalled the app or its token rotated) — verified by reading the full method, there is no token-removal logic anywhere in this 183-line file. Since `POST /me/device-token` (`app/routers/auth.py:312-329`) only ever appends/dedupes tokens and nothing ever removes a bad one, `user.device_tokens` grows monotonically for the lifetime of the account, wasting an FCM call on every send to every dead token forever and never self-healing. Fix: after `send_each`, iterate `response.responses` alongside the original `messages`/tokens list, collect tokens whose result failed with an unregistered/invalid-argument error, and remove them from `user.device_tokens` in the same commit. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Add `DELETE /api/auth/me/device-token` so a logged-out device stops receiving another user's pushes — `app/routers/auth.py` has `POST /me/device-token` (line 312) to register a token but no corresponding way to remove one; verified via grep for "device-token"/"device_tokens" across `app/routers/` and `app/services/` that no endpoint or logout path ever calls `current_user.device_tokens = [...]` to strip an entry. On a shared or resold device, or simply when a user logs out and a different person logs into the same app install, the old user's device token remains in their `device_tokens` list and keeps receiving that old user's bill-alert pushes indefinitely — a real, live privacy/correctness gap for a native-mobile app with push notifications already shipped on both iOS and Android. Add `DELETE /api/auth/me/device-token` (body or query: `device_token`) mirroring the exact list-filter pattern already used by the POST handler's de-dupe step (`tokens = [t for t in tokens if t.get("token") != token_data.device_token]`), and a matching router test. (Wiring the iOS/Android logout flows to actually call it is a natural mobile follow-up once this exists, deliberately left out of scope here.) {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Wire the already-built `MobileGapAnalyzer` + `mobile_feature_map` static data into a real admin endpoint, instead of leaving it dead — `app/services/mobile_gap_analyzer.py`'s `analyze_mobile_gaps(ios_features, android_features)` (lines 68-99, prioritized iOS-vs-Android feature-parity diffing, already unit-tested in `tests/test_mobile_gap_analyzer.py`) and `app/schemas/mobile_feature_map.py`'s `IOS_FEATURES`/`ANDROID_FEATURES` constants (a real inventory of `billwatch-ios/Sources/Views` and `billwatch-android/.../ui/screens`, per that file's own docstring, dated to the 2026-09-09 mobile-parity research) are both fully-written, tested, pure logic — but verified via repo-wide grep for `mobile_gap_analyzer`/`mobile_feature_map`/`MobileGapAnalyzer` across `app/routers/` and `app/main.py` that NEITHER is imported by any router; only their own test files reference them. Add `GET /api/admin/mobile-gaps` to the existing `app/routers/admin.py` (reusing its established `Depends(require_admin)` pattern, e.g. line 22) that calls `analyze_mobile_gaps(IOS_FEATURES["views"], ANDROID_FEATURES["screens"])` and returns the prioritized gap list, and refresh the two static feature lists against the current file_tree (e.g. add iOS's `AdvancedAnalyticsView`/`RecommendationsView`/`AICoachingView`/`CareerHistoryTimelineView` and Android's `PaywallScreen`/`TopicsScreen`/`AdvancedFilterScreen` if not already present) so the tool reflects reality rather than the 2026-09-09 snapshot. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Delete 5 more orphaned dead-code backend services with self-testing-only test suites — verified via repo-wide grep that each of the following has ZERO production callers anywhere in `app/` (only their own class-definition line and a same-named test file reference them), matching the same recurring orphan pattern already found and deleted across ~10 earlier refuel rounds: `app/services/advanced_search.py` (`AdvancedSearchService`/`SearchFilter` — its `full_text_search` always returns a hardcoded empty `{"total": 0, "results": []}`; the REAL, wired-in advanced-search endpoint at `/api/search/advanced` actually calls the separate, real `SearchService.advanced_search` in `app/services/search_service.py:116`, confirmed by reading `app/routers/search.py:70`), `app/services/performance_optimization_service.py` (`PerformanceOptimizationService`, in-memory cache never instantiated by anything), `app/services/realtime_service.py` (`RealTimeUpdateService`, WebSocket pub/sub scaffold with no WebSocket route anywhere), `app/services/batch_operations.py` (`BatchOperationService`, hardcoded-fake bulk-op returns like `{"user_id": user_id, "followed": len(bill_ids), "failed": 0}`), and `app/services/image_service.py` (`ImageService`, thumbnail/URL helper never called from any router). Delete all 5 source files plus their dedicated orphan test files: `tests/test_advanced_search_filter.py`, `tests/test_performance_optimization_service.py`, `tests/test_realtime_service.py`, `tests/test_realtime_service_subscribe.py`, `tests/test_batch_operations.py`, `tests/test_image_service.py`. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

## Research refuel 2026-09-30 (interactive Claude research pass; 12 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Stop bill status from regressing and firing "backwards" follower alerts — bill_sync_service.py:212 derives status from ONLY the single latestAction (determine_bill_status([latest_action]), congress_api.py:443-476, pure substring matching) and _sync_bill overwrites bill.status unconditionally (line ~223); fan_out() in status_change_alert_service.py:83 alerts on ANY old!=new with no rank check. A House-passed bill whose latest action becomes "Received in the Senate." matches no keyword and is written back as "introduced", then "Referred to ... Committee" -> "in_committee", each pushing/emailing followers a wrong "status changed" notification. Fix: compare app/utils/bill_status.status_rank(new) vs old in _sync_bill and keep the higher-ranked status (except vetoed/became_law/dead), and/or guard fan_out to rank-increasing transitions; add regression tests using realistic action texts. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Stop the scheduled bill sync from clobbering sponsor/policy_area and hydrate from the detail endpoint — Congress.gov's list endpoint (/bill/{congress}) returns only congress/type/number/title/latestAction/updateDate (no sponsors, policyArea or introducedDate; see github.com/LibraryOfCongress/api.congress.gov Documentation/BillEndpoint.md), but bill_sync_service._sync_bill (lines ~185-225) does bill.sponsor_bioguide_id/sponsor_name/sponsor_party/sponsor_state = None and bill.policy_area = bill_data.get("policyArea", {}).get("name") (None) on every update, and creates new bills with those fields null. Policy-area topic following (routers/topics.py), recommendations and policy_areas alerts all key off Bill.policy_area. Also BillSyncService.sync_bill_details (actions/cosponsors/summary, line 259) is never called from any job, router or script, so BillAction rows and bill.summary are never populated by the scheduler. Fix: only assign those fields when present, and have sync_recent_bills call get_bill detail (bounded per run) for new/changed bills to fill sponsor/policy_area/introduced_date and call sync_bill_details. Add tests with list-shaped payloads. {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}

## Auto-research 2026-10-02 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 4 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix `digest_service.run_batch()` silently emailing users with `email_enabled=False` — `app/services/digest_service.py:311-338` checks `prefs.get("digest_frequency") == frequency` but never guards on `prefs.get("email_enabled")`; `send_digest_for_user()` (line 282) only checks `if not user.email`. Default `notification_prefs` on new registration is `{"email_enabled": False, "digest_frequency": "daily"}` (`app/models/user.py:81-86`), so every newly registered user receives daily digest emails before opting in. `tests/test_digest_service.py:42` always creates users with `email_enabled: True`, masking the bug. Fix: add `if not prefs.get("email_enabled"): continue` in `run_batch` alongside the frequency check; add a test with `email_enabled=False` asserting `stats["sent"] == 0`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fix IDOR in `GET /api/export/exports/{export_id}` — `app/routers/data_export.py:48-64` calls `service.get_export_status(numeric_id)` and returns the result (including `user_id` and `download_url`) without checking `status["user_id"] == current_user.id`; any authenticated user can read any other user's export record by guessing sequential integer IDs. `list_exports` correctly filters by user_id (line 56-73) but `get_export_status` does not. Fix: add `if status["user_id"] != current_user.id: raise HTTPException(status_code=403, detail="Forbidden")` after line 62 in the router; add a test asserting a second user's request gets 403 on the first user's export. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `register()` never records a SecurityAuditService audit event — `app/routers/auth.py:138-168` creates new accounts and issues JWTs with no call to `SecurityAuditService`, while `login()` (line 182) records both `EVENT_LOGIN_SUCCESS` and `EVENT_LOGIN_FAILED`. Account creation is a security-relevant event for abuse tracking and audit compliance. The service is already imported (line 18) and `client_ip`/`user_agent` helpers already available in `security_audit_service.py`. Fix: instantiate `SecurityAuditService(db)` in `register()` after commit and call `await audit.record(EVENT_LOGIN_SUCCESS, user_id=user.id, ip_address=client_ip(request), agent=user_agent(request))` before returning; add a test asserting an `AuthEvent` row is created on registration. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] `PATCH /me/settings` accepts arbitrary `notification_prefs` dict, bypassing `digest_frequency` format validation — `app/routers/auth.py:313-333` sets `current_user.notification_prefs = payload.notification_prefs` (raw `dict | None`, schema `app/schemas/auth.py:80-86`), while `PATCH /me/notifications` (lines 279-297) validates `digest_frequency` via `NotificationPrefsUpdate.digest_frequency = Field(None, pattern="^(daily|weekly|none)$")` (`app/schemas/auth.py:72`). A user who POSTs `{"notification_prefs": {"digest_frequency": "hourly", "email_enabled": true}}` via `/me/settings` silently breaks digest delivery: `run_batch()` compares `prefs["digest_frequency"] == frequency` (line 329) — no scheduler frequency ever equals "hourly", so the user stops receiving digests with no error or UI feedback. Also replaces the entire dict, losing any prefs keys not included in the payload. Fix: validate `notification_prefs` contents (coerce or reject invalid `digest_frequency`) in the PATCH /me/settings handler; add a test confirming invalid frequency is rejected or normalized. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Auto-research 2026-10-17 (ovn_auto_research.sh; 3 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Allowlist `sort_by` on GET /api/bills and GET /api/legislators to prevent 500 crashes — `bills.py:131` uses `getattr(Bill, sort_by, Bill.latest_action_date)` and `legislators.py:99` uses `getattr(Legislator, sort_by, Legislator.last_name)` with the raw user-supplied string; passing a relationship name (`sort_by=actions`, `sort_by=cosponsors`, `sort_by=votes`) retrieves a relationship descriptor that SQLAlchemy cannot sort by, crashing with a 500 on public unauthenticated endpoints — any anonymous caller can trigger this. Fix: add an explicit allowlist (e.g. `{"id", "title", "introduced_date", "latest_action_date", "status", "policy_area"}` for bills; similar for legislators) and silently fall back to the default column for any unrecognised value. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Gate `?regenerate=true` on GET /api/analytics/{bill_id}/summary behind auth to stop anonymous callers burning paid Claude API — `analytics.py:99-126` passes `regenerate=True` to `BillSummaryService.regenerate_summary()` (`bill_summary_service.py:130`) which deletes the cached `BillSummary` row and calls `self.client.messages.create(model="claude-sonnet-4-6", ...)` (a real, paid Anthropic API call); the endpoint has no `Depends(get_current_user)` and no `@limiter.limit()`, so any anonymous user can spam it to exhaust Anthropic quota and destroy the summary cache. Fix: add `Depends(get_current_user)` and `@limiter.limit("5/minute")` to the endpoint; optionally move regeneration to an admin-only POST route. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Fix web token-refresh interceptor's `/auth/` guard forcing logout when access token expires on `/auth/me` — `api.ts:47` checks `error.config?.url?.includes('/auth/')` to decide whether to skip the automatic token-refresh-and-retry logic; this correctly skips `/auth/refresh` itself, but also skips every other auth-namespaced call including `/auth/me` (called in `auth.ts:70` on page load and after login), `/auth/me/address`, and `/auth/me/settings` — so when the 30-min access token expires while any of those requests are in flight, the interceptor skips the refresh and immediately logs the user out instead of silently renewing. Fix: narrow the guard to `url?.includes('/auth/refresh')` (or an explicit path equality check) so only the refresh endpoint itself is excluded. {cat: web; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-22 02:22 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens performance; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot d7e387c37f, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix N+1 query in _hydrate_bill_details for action deduplication — `billwatch-backend/app/services/bill_sync_service.py:136` (`_hydrate_bill_details`) The function executes a separate database query for each action to check if it already exists, resulting in N+1 queries where N is the number of actions returned by the API (up to 50). This causes significant latency during bill synchronization. Evidence: `existing = await db.execute( select(BillAction).where( BillAction.bill_id == bill.id, BillAction.action_date == action_date,`. Fix: Fetch all existing BillActions for the bill in a single query before processing the new actions, then check for duplicates in Python using a set of (action_date, description) tuples. Add billwatch-backend/tests/test_hydrate_bill_details_n1.py to verify the fix. VERIFY: `cd billwatch-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_hydrate_bill_details_n1.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-22 09:37 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens test-gaps; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 4f84bf055b, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Fix set_cached to use per-entry TTL instead of mutating global cache_ttl — `billwatch-backend/app/services/performance_optimization_service.py:31` (`set_cached`) Calling set_cached with a custom ttl (e.g., 60 seconds) permanently changes the global cache_ttl attribute to 60, causing all other cached entries to expire after 60 seconds instead of their intended 1 hour, leading to excessive cache misses and backend load. Evidence: `self.cache[key] = value self.cache_timestamps[key] = datetime.now() if ttl: self.cache_ttl = ttl`. Fix: Store the TTL per entry in a new dictionary (e.g., self.cache_ttls) and use that specific TTL in get_cached for expiration checks. Add billwatch-backend/tests/test_performance_optimization_service.py to verify that setting a short TTL on one key does not affect the expiration of other keys. VERIFY: `cd billwatch-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_performance_optimization_service.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Qwen explore 2026-10-27 12:18 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens performance; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot 82c162d4eb, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] check_alerts_for_bill loads all active alerts into memory and iterates in Python — `billwatch-backend/app/services/bill_alert_service.py:142` (`check_alerts_for_bill`) The method loads every active alert from the database into memory and then iterates over them in Python to check keyword matches. As the number of users and their alerts grows, this list becomes arbitrarily large, causing high memory usage and slow matching for each bill processed. Evidence: `alerts_stmt = select(BillAlert).where(BillAlert.is_active == True) alerts_result = await self.session.execute(alerts_stmt) alerts =`. Fix: Push the keyword matching logic into the SQL query using ILIKE conditions on Bill.title and Bill.summary, so only matching alerts are returned from the database. Add billwatch-backend/tests/test_alert_matching_efficiency.py to verify the query includes ILIKE filters rather than loading all alerts. VERIFY: `cd billwatch-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_alert_matching_efficiency.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}

- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Use SQL aggregation instead of loading all votes to count positions — `billwatch-backend/app/services/legislator_statistics_service.py:39` (`get_legislator_statistics`) The query loads every individual vote row for the legislator into Python memory to count positions, which scales linearly with the number of roll calls (thousands) instead of using a single SQL GROUP BY aggregation. Evidence: `votes = ( await self.db.execute( select(Vote.position).where(Vote.legislator_id == legislator_id) ) ).scalars().all() yea = sum(1 for p in votes if p`. Fix: Replace the scalar load and Python sums with a single SQL query using func.count() and case() expressions to aggregate Yea/Nay/Present/Not Voting counts in one database round-trip. Add billwatch-backend/tests/test_legislator_stats_perf.py to verify the aggregation returns correct counts without loading all rows. ## Qwen explore 2026-11-01 05:50 (ovn_qwen_explore.py; lens bug-hunt; 1 item(s) proposed by the local 27B; file, symbol and quote verified in the snapshot f87568de6c, VERIFY red-before; review)
- [ ] [P4] [decomposed] Fix NameError in RequestIdMiddleware when call_next raises — `billwatch-backend/app/core/request_id_middleware.py:33` (`dispatch`) If 'call_next(request)' raises an exception, the 'finally' block executes but 'response' is never assigned. The subsequent line 'response.headers[...]' then raises a 'NameError', masking the original exception and breaking error propagation. Evidence: `try: response = await call_next(request) finally: request_id_ctx.reset(token) response.headers["X-Request-ID"] = request_id`. Fix: Move the header assignment inside the 'try' block immediately after 'call_next' succeeds, or wrap the 'call_next' call in a try/except that re-raises after resetting context. Add billwatch-backend/tests/test_request_id_middleware.py to verify exception propagation. VERIFY: `cd billwatch-backend && python3 -m pytest tests/test_request_id_middleware.py -q`. [src:qwen-explore] {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

## Seeded round 5 2026-11-04 (Mark-authorized)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Make bill and legislator search honor the LIKE escaping they already apply — `search_bills` in `billwatch-backend/app/routers/bills.py` (lines ~112-114 title/short_title/summary and ~129 policy_area) and `search_legislators` in `billwatch-backend/app/routers/legislators.py` (lines ~82-84) pass `sanitize_sql_like(query)` (`app/core/validators.py:397`, which turns `%`/`_` into `\%`/`\_`) into `.ilike(f"%{safe}%")` WITHOUT `escape="\\"`, unlike `app/services/search_service.py` which does pass `escape='\\'`; on any backend with no default escape char (the sqlite test DB) a search for `100%` matches nothing, and `sanitize_sql_like` also never escapes a literal backslash so `a\b` is mangled. Fix: in `sanitize_sql_like` replace `\` with `\\` FIRST, then `%` and `_`; add `escape="\\"` to every `.ilike(...)` that consumes it in those two routers. Keep existing `tests/test_validators_comprehensive.py::TestSqlLikeSanitization` passing. Add NEW `billwatch-backend/tests/test_like_escape_routers.py`: seed bills titled `100% funding` and `1000 funding` via the conftest db fixtures, GET `/api/bills?query=100%25` must return exactly the first; `/api/legislators?query=` with a `_` in a seeded name must not match a different one-character-different name; and `sanitize_sql_like("a\\b%") == "a\\\\b\\%"`. VERIFY: `cd billwatch-backend && python -m pytest tests/test_like_escape_routers.py tests/test_validators_comprehensive.py -q --no-cov`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Give GET /api/bills a stable, NULLS-LAST sort — `search_bills` in `billwatch-backend/app/routers/bills.py` (lines ~140-145) does `order_by(sort_column.desc())` on columns like `latest_action_date`/`introduced_date` that are nullable and non-unique with no tiebreaker, so on Postgres (NULLs sort FIRST on DESC) undated bills lead page 1, and ties across `page`/`page_size` OFFSET pages can duplicate or drop rows; `get_trending_bills` (line ~193, `order_by(Bill.latest_action_date.desc())`) has the same missing tiebreak. Create `billwatch-backend/app/utils/sort_order.py` with `order_clauses(column, tiebreak_column, descending: bool) -> list` returning `[column.desc().nulls_last(), tiebreak.desc()]` (or `.asc().nulls_last()` / `tiebreak.asc()`), use it in `search_bills` (tiebreak `Bill.id`) and add `Bill.id.desc()` after the existing order in `get_trending_bills`. Add NEW `billwatch-backend/tests/test_sort_order.py`: compile `select(Bill).order_by(*order_clauses(Bill.latest_action_date, Bill.id, True))` with `sqlalchemy.dialects.postgresql.dialect()` and assert the string contains `NULLS LAST` and `bills.id DESC` in that order, the asc variant has `ASC NULLS LAST`, and a sqlite end-to-end GET `/api/bills?sort_by=latest_action_date` with 3 identical-date bills returns them in descending id order on both pages when `page_size=2`. VERIFY: `cd billwatch-backend && python -m pytest tests/test_sort_order.py tests/test_bills.py -q --no-cov`. {cat: backend; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}

- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Fix `format_currency_short` unit rollover and negative small amounts — `format_currency_short` in `billwatch-backend/app/utils/finance_math.py` formats `999_999` as `$1000.0K`, `999_950_000` as `$1000.0M` (rounds to 1 decimal AFTER choosing the unit) and `-5` as `$-5.00` (sign inside the `$` because the sub-1000 branch formats the signed `amount`); verified by running it. Fix: compute the rounded value, and if `round(value, 1) >= 1000` promote to the next unit (K->M->B; B stays), build the sign prefix once (`-$`) for every branch including the sub-1000 `,.2f` path, and return `"$0"` for NaN/inf.
